# To the Kind Reader.

Scholarly matters <term>Gelehrte Sachen: matters of academic or theological study</term> belong to learned people, for whose sake such things are published in the world. Learned people seek learned books so that they may become even more learned through them. It is indisputable that no more learned book can be found than the divine <term>Holy Scripture</term>. For wisdom has truth as its foundation, upon which the former is built as the most beautiful edifice. Since the Holy Scripture <note>the Bible</note> thus encompasses both divine and natural <term>wisdom</term> <note>original: "Weisheit"</note>, I may surely venture—with this little work (which is grounded upon the same, and which I have compiled and prepared according to my modest <term>knowledge</term> of the ancient <term>Hebrew language</term>)—to [present it] <note>The original German sentence breaks off here; the catchword "ter" suggests the next page begins with "unter," meaning "among" or "to."</note> to the learned world...

<summary>In this preface, the author justifies the publication of his work by arguing that scholarly books are essential for the pursuit of wisdom, identifying the Holy Scripture as the ultimate source of all knowledge.</summary>
<keywords>preface, scholarship, Holy Scripture, wisdom, truth, Hebrew language, theology</keywords>

--- Page 6 ---
<meta>This page continues the sentence from the previous page, beginning with the second half of the word "unter" (among/before), following the catchword "ter."</meta>

to present it before their eyes; so that I may faithfully share with others that light which I have received in such knowledge <term>Wissenschaft: here meaning a specialized field of study or branch of learning</term>, in the confidence that those who possess a greater light than I do will be spurred on to likewise bestow such [insight] upon me and other faithful lovers of the art <term>Kunst: referring to the specific discipline or craft discussed in this book</term>. Therefore, everyone is politely requested to go through this little treatise <term>Tractätchen</term> of mine thoroughly; I then do not doubt that in many places the light of truth will dawn upon him, and thus my work will find the desired approval <term>Approbation</term>, which may draw the reader's valuable favor toward me. Farewell and enjoy! <note>original Latin: "Vale et fruere!"</note>

<summary>The author concludes his preface by expressing his desire to share his intellectual "light" with others, hoping his treatise encourages a mutual exchange of knowledge among scholars and gains the reader's approval.</summary>
<keywords>scholarship, light of truth, treatise, mutual learning, knowledge, reader's favor</keywords>

# Introduction.

->VAH. Isaiah 4:13. <note>original Latin: "Benedictus DEVS SPIRITVS IEHOVA." Note: While the text cites Isaiah 4:13, that chapter only contains six verses; this may be a printer's error for Amos 4:13, which describes God's spirit and creative power.</note><-

He created man in His own image, in the image of God He created him. Genesis 1:27. He has distinguished him from other irrational animals <term>unvernünftigen Thieren: literally "un-reasoning animals," a standard philosophical distinction of the era marking the human soul as unique due to its capacity for logic and divine connection</term> through the intellect of his immortal soul; He has given him the commandments of God and the law of Moses on Mount Sinai. Yes, finally the Law of Grace <term>Gnadengesetz: a theological concept referring to the Gospel or the New Covenant, which offers salvation through divine grace rather than just the strict adherence to the Mosaic Law</term>, of which the prophet Isaiah (2:3) prophesied: *For out of Zion shall go forth the law.* For out of Zion shall go forth the law. <note>original Hebrew: כִּי מִצִיּוֹן תֵּצֵא תוֹרָה (Ki mitziyon tetze torah)</note> Namely, through the second person <note>A reference to Jesus Christ as the Second Person of the Holy Trinity</note>, Jesus of Nazareth, who redeemed us and [has] made us children of God

which He has [given], with the addition that we should walk in His way, on the path of life and light, which is the divine will. Of this the great Apostle and Evangelist John speaks thus in John 1:12–13: 
> But as many as received Him, to them He gave power to become children of God, who are born of God. 

Now, such a will is fulfilled in us through knowledge <term>Erkenntniß: In this context, it refers to more than just intellectual learning; it is a deep, spiritual recognition or experiential understanding of the divine</term>, namely: when we possess divine knowledge, we also seek to reconcile our own will with the divine will. Christ Jesus our Savior teaches us the same in a prayer, where He speaks: 
> Our Father, who art in the heavens, hallowed [be Thy name] etc. Thy kingdom come to us. 

The true, actual knowledge or contemplation of God, which is called the <term>beatific vision: the ultimate direct self-communication of God to the individual soul, often considered the highest state of happiness in heaven</term> by theologians; Thy will be done, etc. Through knowledge, it happens that we fulfill the divine will. The great royal Prophet David likewise gives us this to understand, when he says to his son in 1 Chronicles 28:9: <note>original: "1. Paralipom." is the traditional Latin title for the Books of Chronicles.</note>
> Acknowledge the God of your father and serve Him. 

Ah! I remained in astonishment at and over this command; I asked myself: How can I know this divine Omnipotence? Whose form no human will be able to see and live. *“You cannot see my face; for no human shall see me and live.”* <note>original Latin: "Non poteris videre faciem meam; non enim videbit me homo et vivet."</note> Exodus 33:20. <note>The original text cites Exodus 30:20, but this is a likely error for 33:20, which contains this specific phrase.</note> The infinite attributes of God are likewise impossible to grasp with a finite human understanding,

has given us, with the addition that we should walk in His way—on the path of life and light, which is the divine will. Of this the great apostle and evangelist John speaks thus in John 1:12–13: "But as many as received Him, to them He gave power to become children of God, who are born of God." Now, such a will is realized within us through knowledge <term>Erkenntniß: in this context, it refers to a deep, spiritual understanding or realization of divine truth rather than mere factual learning</term>, in this manner: when we possess divine knowledge, we also seek to align our own will with the divine will.

Christ Jesus our Savior teaches us the same in a prayer, where He says: Our Father, who art in heaven, hallowed be, etc. Thy kingdom come—the true, essential knowledge or contemplation of God, which is called by theologians the *beatific vision* <note>original Latin: *Visio beatifica* — the ultimate direct self-communication of God to the individual person</note>; Thy will be done, etc. Through this knowledge, it comes to pass that we fulfill the divine will.

The great royal prophet David likewise gives us this to understand, where he says to his son in 1 Chronicles <term>Paralipom.: short for Paralipomenon, the traditional title for the Books of Chronicles used in the Douay-Rheims and older Bibles</term> 28:9: "Know the God of your father and serve Him."

Ah! At and over this command I remained in wonder; I asked myself: How can I know this divine omnipotence? Whose form no human will be able to see and live. "You cannot see my face; for man shall not see me and live." <note>original Latin: *Non poteris videre faciem meam; non enim videbit me homo et viuet.*</note> Exodus 33:20 <note>The text cites Exodus 30, but the passage is located in chapter 33.</note>. It is likewise not possible to grasp the infinite attributes of God with a finite human understanding,

--- Page 9 ---
<meta>This page continues the thought from the previous page regarding the impossibility of seeing God's face, noting that the divine nature is neither to be comprehended nor fathomed by human means. The catchword "sange" at the bottom of the page likely refers to the completion of the word "Umfange" (scope/extent) on the following page.</meta>

neither to be comprehended, nor to be fathomed. I sought comfort from this very same royal prophet, David, and found him likewise sighing in this contemplation: Psalm 8:1. O Lord! How wonderful is Your name over all the earth; for Your glory is above the heavens. Yet he comforted me, as he gave me the answer, as it were, in the 19th chapter and 2nd verse: The heavens tell of the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands. As if he wished to say: Through the knowledge of nature and creation <note>original: "Kreatur" — here referring to all created things or the natural world</note>, we can attain the knowledge of God. 

But I am satisfied even more by the golden words of the great Apostle to the World <note>original: "Weltapostels" — a title for Paul as the apostle to the Gentiles/nations</note>, Paul, in the Epistle to the Romans 1:20, where his enlightened spirit speaks thus: *For the invisible things of Him (God) are understood by being seen through the creation of the world, through the things that are made.* <note>original Latin: "Invisibila enim ipsius (DEI) a creatura mundi, per ea, quae facta sunt, intellectu conspiciuntur."</note> That is: through the visible, we must recognize the invisible. And this is confirmed by what God Himself said to Moses in Exodus 33:23 with the following words: And I will take away my hands, so you shall see what is behind me; but my face shall not be seen. 

Wherein many highly enlightened interpreters of the Holy Scriptures have understood nature, along with its effects and properties, to be represented by these "hinder parts" <note>original: "hintern Theil"</note>. Following this hint, in order to arrive at divine knowledge <term>Erkenntniß: In this context, it refers to more than just intellectual learning; it is a deep, spiritual recognition or experiential understanding of the divine</term>, I turned to the physical or natural sciences, which in their entire scope—

<summary>The author reflects on the mystery of God's nature, concluding that while God's face cannot be seen, He can be known through the "hinder parts" of creation—that is, the study of nature and the physical sciences.</summary>
<keywords>King David, Psalms, Apostle Paul, Romans 1:20, Moses, Exodus 33, natural theology, divine knowledge, visible and invisible, study of nature</keywords>

sole focus and aim. However, as to where 
such a thing might be found, I sought to inquire 
with all diligence among various scholars. 
While I, on the other hand, did not know how 
to find the path to this, and looked about 
with a very troubled heart and soul, behold! 
a man stepped forward in royal finery, 
greeted me with the kiss of peace (Solomon <note>original Hebrew: "Schelomo"</note>), 
showed me a book, and said: Here you have 
a song that I have composed; take your time 
and contemplate it well, and so you shall find the 
way to the true philosophy. Falling to my 
knees, I asked him: What must I do so that 
I may rightly understand the meaning of this song? 
He then addressed me thus: Know that 
Abraham, my ancient forefather, left behind 
a book called *Yetzirah* <note>original: "Jezirah" — referring to the *Sefer Yetzirah* or "Book of Formation," a foundational text of Jewish mysticism and alchemy</note>; within it you will find 
a number which was most dear to him, 
which I also cited in my *Proverbs* <note>original Latin: "proverbiis" — referring to the biblical Book of Proverbs, traditionally attributed to Solomon</note>. 
Take this concentrated number singly and doubly; 
draw a single number from the double, and so 
you shall have found that which you seek—and 
all in all—and you will understand my song. 
Regarding this parabolic instruction from the 
wise King, I took counsel with myself and 
went to great lengths to discover the number. 
At last, I found this Book of *Yetzirah*; I searched 
for the beloved number of the ancient patriarch

<meta>This page continues the narrator's quest to decode a riddle given by King Solomon on the previous page regarding the "beloved number" 7 from the Book of Formation (Sefer Yetzirah). <page-num>9</page-num>
<sig>A 5</sig>

-><image-desc>Decorative horizontal printer's ornament featuring a central bar flanked by two floral/star motifs.</image-desc><-

he (understand God) loved the number seven above all things that are under heaven. I doubled this number, so I obtained 14 as the result <note>original: "facit"</note>; I counted the number out perhaps a hundred times, and did not know what was meant to be understood by it, until it finally occurred to me that <note>original Hebrew: "זהב"</note> Gold <note>original: "Sahab" — a phonetic spelling of the Hebrew word 'Zahav'. In Gematria, the letters Zayin (7), He (5), and Bet (2) sum to 14</note> has the very same number. I sought further and found: if I make one out of this obtained number (namely 14) (understand: adding them together, or concentrating them), I obtain 5 as the result (as 4 and 1 is 5). I pondered this with great diligence, and it occurred to me: that the <term>Fifth Essence</term> <note>original Latin: "Quinta Essentia" — the "quintessence," or the pure, celestial substance that permeates all nature, beyond the four physical elements</note> was understood by it; and because the <term>Sun</term> <note>original Latin: "Sol" — in alchemy, the Sun represents Gold</note> carries a circle with a center point <note>The alchemical symbol for the Sun/Gold is ☉</note> as its sign, this wise man [Solomon] has infallibly meant the circle (Gold) with the doubled number and the center (the Fifth Essence) with the concentrated or single number. Here I was indeed delighted, but I did not yet know what significance the single number 7 had.

And as I now went about quite deeply immersed in these thoughts, I encountered a Job, who was leading his daughter by the hand; he asked me the reason why I was so pensive? I answered this patient man <note>The biblical Job is famously known for his patience</note> that I had been directed by a wise Solomon in a riddle to a beloved number of 7, which I did not understand. This dear old man replied to me: I likewise love this number, and have therefore (pointing to his daughter) [named] my dearest, most beautiful, and youngest daughter after

<keywords>Gematria, Gold, Zahav, Fifth Essence, Sun symbol, Solomon, Job, alchemy, numerology, Seven</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the narrator's investigation into the mystical number 7. On the previous page, he decoded the number 14 as "Gold" and the number 5 as the "Fifth Essence." Now, he seeks the specific meaning of 7 by analyzing a biblical name using Hebrew numerology.</meta>

<page-num>10</page-num>

->— * ——— * —<-

this number called **Keren-happuch** <note>original Hebrew: קֶרֶן הַפּוּךְ. In the Book of Job 42:14, this is the name of Job’s third daughter. It is traditionally translated as "Horn of Kohl" or "Horn of Eye-paint."</note>. Then, for the first time, the light dawned on me. I found that a precious stone bears the very same name **Puch** <note>original Hebrew: פוך</note>, which in the <term>great number</term> <note>the full numerical value of the Hebrew letters: Pe (80) + Vav (6) + Kaph (20) = 106</note> contains 106, but in the <term>small number</term> <note>the reduced sum of the digits (1+0+6) used in Gematria</note> contains **7** (that is, when it is concentrated into a single number). I contemplated the entire name of this maiden and found 7 letters. Finally, it occurred to me that something else was hidden behind these names, and I was not mistaken; after I placed other <term>points</term> <note>vowel markings or 'niqqud' added to Hebrew consonants to change their pronunciation and meaning</note> under these names, as can be seen here, instead of **Keren-happuch** <note>original: קֶרֶן הַפּוּךְ</note> I brought out **Koran-happuch** <note>original: קֹרֶן הַפּוּךְ</note>, which is to say: *The Puch has become enlightened and radiant.* <note>The narrator is punning on the Hebrew root 'Karan,' meaning to shine or radiate—the same word used to describe Moses’ face glowing after descending from Sinai.</note> I thanked the dear old man and left him to reflect further on the matter; I sat down with the Holy Scriptures and went through the lives and deeds of the patriarchs and prophets; there I found various traces <note>original Latin: *vestigia*</note> that they possessed this science, and I was in the process of gathering such scriptural passages and interpreting them <term>physico-cabalistically</term> <note>original Latin: *physico-cabalistice* — a method of interpretation that blends the study of nature (physics/alchemy) with Jewish mystical tradition (Kabbalah).</note>, when a voice addressed me thus: "What are you trying to do? Do you not know that the **Doctors of the Church** <note>original Latin: *Doctores Ecclesiae*</note> and the **interpreters of Holy Scripture** <note>original Latin: *interpretes sacrae scripturae*</note> have understood it in a different sense? How do you dare to interpret such things as referring to natural matters?" I recovered myself and gave this answer: it is quite well known to me that our holy teachers of the Church [interpreted] the Holy Scripture in a **historical sense**, or in a **mystical sense** <note>original Latin: *sensu historico, vel in sensu mystico*</note>, or spiritual understanding—

<summary>The narrator uses Hebrew gematria to link the biblical name Keren-happuch to the number 7 and alchemical radiance, subsequently defending his "physico-cabalistic" method of interpreting the Bible against traditional theological views.</summary>
<keywords>Keren-happuch, Gematria, Puch, Physico-cabalistica, Alchemical Bible interpretation, Number 7, Biblical radiance</keywords>

--- Page 13 ---
<meta>This page continues the author’s defense of his interpretive method. On the previous page, he described finding alchemical secrets hidden within the Hebrew names of the Bible, specifically concerning the daughter of Job, Keren-happuch, and the number 7.</meta>

<page-num>11</page-num>

->*—*<-

understood and interpreted. I do not contest this interpretation in the slightest; rather, I revere it with my mouth and heart as a holy interpretation used by Christ and the Apostles themselves. I hold it to be a guiding rule of the true faith. These <term>modes of explaining or interpreting the Holy Scriptures</term> <note>original Latin: *modi explanandi vel interpretandi sacram scripturam*</note> are called **universal** <note>original Latin: *universales*</note> by theologians. However, among the "modes of explaining," three others are permitted by theologians as **particular** <note>original Latin: *particulares*</note> ones. *See the "Holy Library" by Sixtus of Siena, page 171, book 3.* Namely: **Elementary**, or **Kabbalistic**; **Physical**; and **Prophetic**. Consequently, such a "mode of explaining" has been expressly permitted to me by the Christian Catholic Church. I pursued my work with the greatest diligence, going through the Holy Scriptures as they were given in their mother tongue (Hebrew), along with the Chaldean interpreter (<term>Targum</term> <note>An ancient Aramaic translation and commentary on the Hebrew Bible.</note>). The texts of those I found to be possessors of the Art, I have explained **Kabbalistically-physically** <note>original: *cabalistico-physice*. A method that looks for chemical or natural secrets hidden within the numerical value and mystical meaning of Hebrew letters.</note>. From this, I have described the greatest secret of nature, namely the **Hermetic Mastery** <note>original: *Magisterium Hermeticum*. The "Great Work" of alchemy, often referring to the creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>. In this matter, highly learned men have preceded me with their example in Hebrew, such as: **Philo the Jew**, **Flavius Josephus**, **Clement of Alexandria**, **Jerome**, and **Pico della Mirandola**. Among the Latin authors, however, I especially followed **Vincent of Beauvais** in his *Mirror of Nature* <note>original: *Speculo naturali*. A massive 13th-century encyclopedia that organized all knowledge of the natural world.</note>. Therefore, I say to those **theologians**, along with the excellently learned **Michael Psellus** <note>An 11th-century Byzantine monk and scholar known for his works on philosophy and demonology.</note>—

<page-num>12</page-num>

To Xiphilinus, Patriarch of Constantinople, in Michael Maier’s book 3 of *The Golden Table* <note>original: *Aureae Mensae*. A famous alchemical work by Michael Maier, published in 1617, which traces the history of alchemy through various nations and figures.</note>: "To you more excellent and higher things have been entrusted; but I lie upon the ground and am a contemplator of nature," etc. That is: To you, more noble and higher things have been entrusted; but I lie upon the earth and am an observer of nature.

I will therefore proceed to the work of lighting a light for you, my kind reader, in the hope that it may provide the godly with a **Splendor of Light** <note>original Hebrew: *Or Nogah*; original Latin: *Splendorem Lucis*. A phrase often used in mystical literature to describe divine radiance.</note> and lead you into the Garden of Paradise, which is likewise signified by the words **Splendor of Light** <note>original Hebrew: *Or Nogah*</note> when they are transposed as an **anagram** <note>A wordplay technique common in Kabbalah where letters are rearranged to reveal hidden meanings.</note>. For from the word **Light** <note>original Hebrew: *Or* (אור)</note> arises the word **See** <note>original Hebrew: *Reu* (ראו)</note>, and from the word **Splendor** <note>original Hebrew: *Nogah* (נגה)</note> the word **the Garden** <note>original Hebrew: *Hagon* (הגן)</note>; that is: **See the Garden.** Finally, you will also find in the little word **Light** <note>original Hebrew: *Or*</note>—which is written in the original language with three letters, namely: **Aleph** (א), which signifies A; **Vav** <note>OCR says "Waf"</note> (ו), which is a W; and **Resh** (ר), which indicates an R—the letters that begin my name: **A**, **W**; while the **R**, which closes the word, is also the conclusion of my name <note>The author, Abraham von Worms, is pointing out that the letters of the Hebrew word for light (A-W-R) correspond to his own name or initials.</note>.

May the Most High grant me the grace of the Holy Spirit, so that I may light this light for the glory of God and the benefit of my neighbor.

<meta>Continuing from the reference table on the previous page, this section defines the specific methods of numerological calculation (Gematria) that will be used throughout the text. The catchword "Drit-" on the previous page likely refers to a "Third" point or example beginning shortly.</meta>

## Note:
<note>original Latin: "Notandum"</note>

That the so-called great, middle, and small numbers appearing in this present book have the following meaning: Namely, 1) a **great number** is called that from which nothing is taken away, and which is left in its original value. For example: <term>Yod</term> <note>the 10th letter of the Hebrew alphabet</note> is 10, and <term>Kaph</term> <note>the 11th letter, with a value of 20</note> is 20. Add these together, and 30 results, and they remain 30. 2) A **middle number** is when, for example, these are put together: <term>Ayin</term>, <term>Chet</term>, <term>Teth</term>, <note>original Latin "id est": that is</note> 70, 8, and 9. Add these, and you have 87; add these 87 placed one above the other, thus: <sup>8</sup>/<sub>7</sub>, and you have 15, which is the middle number. 3) The **small** or **concentrated number** is when you take the aforementioned 15 together, or place them one above the other, thus: <sup>1</sup>/<sub>5</sub>, and you have 6. This number can no longer be reduced, because it consists of only one digit.

### Second Example:

<term>Resh</term>, <term>Tsadi</term>, and <term>He</term> have in their own value 295, which is called the **great number**; place these one above the other, thus: <sup>2</sup>/<sub>9</sub>/<sub>5</sub>, and you have 16, which is called the **middle number**; now place this 16 again one above the other, thus: <sup>1</sup>/<sub>6</sub>, and you have 7, which is called the **small number**.

<summary>This page defines the three levels of Kabbalistic numerology (Gematria) used in the manuscript: the "great" (full value), "middle" (sum of digits), and "small" (reduced single digit) numbers. These methods allow the author to extract multiple layers of symbolic meaning from the same set of Hebrew letters.</summary>

## Note: <note>original Latin: "Notandum"</note>

The so-called large, middle, and small numbers appearing in the present book are understood as follows: Namely, 1) a **large number** is that from which nothing is taken away, and which is left in its original meaning. For example: Yod is 10 and Kaph <note>written here as "Caf"</note> is 20. Add these together and the result is 30, and it remains 30. 2) A **middle number** is when, for example, the following are put together: Ayin, Cheth, Teth, that is: 70, 8, and 9. Add these, and you have 87; place these 87 one above the other to add them, thus:
8
7
so you have 15, which is the **middle number**. 3) The **small or concentrated number** is when you take the above 15 together, or place them one above the other, thus:
1
5
so you have 6. This number can no longer be reduced, because it consists of only one digit. <note>The author is describing the process of "digit summing" or "reduction," a common practice in kabbalistic numerology to find deeper meanings within words.</note>

## Second Example:

Resh, Tsadi, and He have in their own value 295, which is called the **large number**. Place these one above the other, thus:
2
9
5
so you have 16, which is called the **middle number**; now place this 16 again one above the other, thus:
1
6
so you have 7, which is called the **small number**.

# Third:

**Puch** <note>Hebrew: פוך; traditionally meaning antimony or eye-paint, often used in alchemy to represent the raw mineral matter</note> <note>original Latin: "id est"</note> that is: the Matter, which in Hebrew is written only with the letters Pe, Waf, and Caf; its "great number" is 106. For Pe is 80, Caf is 20, and Waf is 6. Place these one above the other <insert>1<br/>0<br/>6</insert>, and you have 7, which is called the "small number."

# Fourth:

**Sahab** <note>Hebrew: זהב; Sahab or Zahav</note> <note>original Latin: "id est"</note> that is: Gold (which in Hebrew is written with only 3 letters, namely: Sain, He, and Beth); its "great number" is 14 (for Sain is 7, He is 5, and Beth is 2). Place these one above the other <insert>1<br/>4</insert>, and you have 5, which is the "small number."

Furthermore, note that by virtue of the two examples cited above, the <term>Philosophical Matter</term> <note>original Latin: "Materia Philosophica"</note> carries the number 7, and Sahab, <term>that is, Gold</term>, has the number 14 in its calculation. From this latter number, however, comes the "small number" 5, which signifies the <term>Quintessence</term> <note>original Latin: "Quintam essentiam"; the "fifth essence" or purest spirit of a substance</note> and is the <term>Center of Mastery</term> <note>original: "Centrum der Meisterschaft"</note>.

# First Chapter.

## Who the Great Hermes is.

Hermes Trismegistus, after whom the entire art and science <note>The "Hermetic Art," another name for alchemy</note> is named, is certainly already well known to all students devoted to this philosophy. Many scholars have written enough concerning him that I consider myself of too little account to place myself by their side. However, I cannot leave untouched here that which Father Athanasius Kircher <note>A famous 17th-century Jesuit scholar and polymath known for his wide-ranging interests in science, Egyptology, and the occult</note>—who was incomparably experienced in both the mathematical sciences and in Oriental languages—cites in his *Egyptian Oedipus* <note>original Latin: "Oedypo Aegyptiaco"; Kircher’s massive work attempting to decipher Egyptian hieroglyphs and explain ancient mysteries</note>, Volume II, Part 2, Class 10, as follows (even though in his *The Underground World* <note>original Latin: "Mundo subterraneo"; a work where Kircher discussed geology and minerals, often expressing skepticism about the practicalities of making gold</note> he looked upon this art with contempt): That the first humans were aware of an art of making gold—that is, of either extracting gold from all substances or of <term>transmuting</term> <note>The alchemical process of changing one substance, usually a "base" metal like lead, into a "noble" metal like gold</note> one metal into another—

<summary>This page introduces Hermes Trismegistus as the foundational figure of alchemy and cites the scholar Athanasius Kircher regarding the ancient origins of the art of gold-making.</summary>
<keywords>Hermes Trismegistus, Athanasius Kircher, alchemy, transmutation, gold-making, Egyptian Oedipus, The Underground World</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the history of alchemy, specifically focusing on the secrecy maintained by Hermes Trismegistus (referred to here as "Mercury") regarding the art of transmutation. and that such knowledge was communicated only to kings, and then to the heads of families <note>original: "Häuptern der Geſchlechter"</note>—not in writing, but only by word of mouth—is so certain that no one could doubt it unless they were a total stranger to the study of history. However, this art has been intentionally buried in profound silence by those who know it, so that the most secret treasury of nature would not be revealed to the ignorant masses <note>original: "unwiſſenden Pöbel"</note>. By keeping it secret, they ensured that the common wealth <note>The "commonweal" or public state</note> would not be plunged into utter ruin and the realm brought to total downfall.

Therefore, not without reason did the wise Mercury <note>The Roman name for Hermes; in this context, he is viewed as the mythical founder of alchemy and ancient wisdom</note>—foreseeing the great damage and disaster that could arise—shroud that excellent part of philosophy <note>original: "Weltweisheit," literally "world-wisdom"</note> dealing with God, the angels, and the world in the darkest symbols, so as not to make it common. In the same way, he rightly withdrew this science—which is the most secret and excellent among those who study the structure of this sub-lunar and subterranean world <note>The "sub-lunar" realm refers to the physical world under the moon subject to change; "subterranean" refers to the minerals and processes beneath the earth</note>—from the reading of the unworthy, hiding it instead beneath hieroglyphic riddles.

From this, it becomes clear that the art is true; and since this Hermes had his seat in

Egypt, and it is well known that the *Emerald Tablet* <note>original: "Tabula Smaragdina," a foundational text of Hermeticism and alchemy attributed to Hermes Trismegistus</note> originates from him; thus there is no doubt at all that this art was common in Egypt, and that Abraham (to whom I now turn) received the art there.

## Chapter Two.

Abraham, the most elect among the patriarchs, whom God the Lord called his most beloved, was—by virtue of his book, the *Jezirah* <note>The *Sefer Yetzirah* or "Book of Formation," the earliest extant book on Jewish esotericism</note> (which is attributed to him)—one of the greatest Kabbalists <note>Practitioners of Kabbalah, a form of Jewish mysticism</note> and natural philosophers <note>original Latin: "Philosophorum naturalium"; in the 18th century, this term referred to those who studied the laws of nature and the physical universe, often including alchemy</note>.

*Eupolemus* <note>A Jewish historian of the 2nd century BCE</note>, an ancient author cited by *Eusebius* in his *Preparation for the Gospel* <note>original: "praepar. euan." (Praeparatio evangelica), a work by the Christian historian Eusebius of Caesarea</note> IX, 17, writes the following words about him: Abraham, who was born at Ur in Babylonia, after he had surpassed everyone in wisdom, invented astrology and other Chaldean magical sciences; he also taught the Phoenicians the rotation of the sun and moon, and other such sciences. This very same thing is confirmed by *Artabanus* in the aforementioned work of *Eusebius*; likewise by *Josephus Flavius* and *Philo Judaeus*. They add that Abraham learned all the Egyptian arts in Egypt and understood them perfectly. And since it is known that *Hermes* had his seat in Egypt, and the Egyptians themselves [of] his... <note>The sentence continues onto the next page</note>

--- Page 24 ---
<meta>This page continues the argument that the secret sciences were practiced in Egypt. The previous page ended with the mention of historians who claimed Abraham learned all the Egyptian arts. This page specifically links Abraham’s biblical wealth and his name change to his mastery of these alchemical secrets.</meta>

...this art <note>The author refers here to alchemy and the Hermetic sciences mentioned on the previous page</note> until the time of the Emperor Diocletian <note>Gaius Aurelius Valerius Diocletianus (reigned 284–305 AD), who famously ordered the burning of Egyptian books on alchemy to prevent them from creating gold and financing rebellions</note>; thus, there is no doubt that Abraham also learned this same art among others. I also find an indication of this in the Divine Holy Scripture, namely in Genesis 13:2, with the following words:

> But Abraham was very burdened with possessions, silver, and gold,

of which it is not mentioned in the said Holy Scripture that he brought such things with him to Egypt; rather, it mentions them for the first time after he had departed from Egypt. Thus, it is not difficult for me to conclude that he acquired such wealth through the art he learned in Egypt. In this opinion I am further strengthened by what is said of him in Genesis 17:5: that he was named Abram, but through the divine blessing he was called Abraham. The verse reads thus:

> And your name shall no longer be Abram; but you shall be called Abraham.

Just as the name of his wife Sarai was also changed by God, in the place cited <note>original Latin abbreviation: "l. c." (loco citato), referring to the same chapter 17</note>, verse 15:

> And God said to Abraham: Sarai, your wife, you shall no longer call Sarai; for Sarah is her name.

Whereupon the blessing of fertility followed in the 16th verse with these words:

Here I find all the interpreters of the Holy Scripture considering what significance the alteration of the two names and the addition of the letter ה <note>The Hebrew letter *He*</note> actually has. If I turn to the Rabbis, I see them likewise speculating on this, and after they have reflected enough, I receive an answer which might indeed seem beautiful to some, but in truth answers almost nothing. It consists of this:

The Rabbis answer me as follows: it is as if God took the last letter of שרי <note>Sarai</note>, which is the י <note>The letter *Yod*</note> and has the numerical value of ten, and from it made two ה <note>The letter *He*</note>, each of which is five in number; of these, He gave one ה <note>He</note> to Abraham, and granted the other ה <note>He</note> to Sarai instead of the י <note>Yod</note>, calling her שרה <note>Sarah</note> instead of שרי <note>Sarai</note>. See <note>original Latin: *Vid.* (vide)</note> Midrash Bereshit Rabbah, chapter 47 <note>A foundational collection of ancient Jewish interpretations of the Book of Genesis</note>. Now, this answer is of no substance for the following reasons <note>original Latin: *rationibus*</note>. For 1) the ה <note>He</note> was added by God to Abraham before Sarah; whereas, according to the opinion of the Rabbis, the ה <note>He</note> would have had to be taken from Sarai first, consequently changing Sarai’s name before the ה <note>He</note> could be bestowed upon Abraham. 2) The Rabbis themselves admit—and most theologians agree, and anyone knowledgeable in the Hebrew language will find in the original text—that Sarai [was changed] through the addition of the ה...

[She had] been blessed. Since no blessing exists without multiplication or increase, yet Sarai’s name (if the Rabbis' opinion were to hold true) would have been diminished—considering she previously contained a י <note>Yod, the tenth letter of the Hebrew alphabet with a numerical value of 10</note> which comprises two הה <note>He-s, each having a value of 5</note>, and now she only had one ה <note>He</note>—it follows naturally from this that such a ה <note>He</note> would not have been an increase, and therefore no blessing, which runs contrary to the truth. 3) In such a way, Abraham would have received the ה <note>He</note> not from God, but from his wife Sarai; yet the original text explicitly states that God gave it to him, and not Sarai. 4) The question arises: what then was improved for this couple by the addition of the ה <note>He</note>? ...to be found in *Bereshit Rabbah* <note>original: *Breschit Raba*; a collection of ancient rabbinic interpretations on the Book of Genesis</note> chapter 12, then in *Song of Songs Rabbah* <note>original: *Schir haschirim Raba*; a rabbinic commentary on the Song of Solomon</note> page 32, as I have previously indicated; even before I properly answer the above question, I find it necessary to explain what root the word "Mandrakes" <note>original: *Mandragorae*</note>—which is called *Dudaim* <note>original: דודאים</note> in the original text—has in Hebrew.

*Dudaim* <note>original: דודאים</note> is derived from *Dod* <note>original: דוד; meaning "beloved" or "uncle"</note>; see Kimchi <note>David Kimhi (1160–1235), a famous medieval rabbi and grammarian</note> as well as Buxtorf <note>Johannes Buxtorf (1564–1629), a notable Hebraist and scholar</note> under the root *Dod* <note>original: דוד</note>; the *im* <note>original: ים</note> denotes the plural. The herb itself is "saturnine" <note>in alchemy, "saturnine" refers to substances or qualities associated with the planet Saturn or the metal lead, often suggesting a cold, heavy, or foundational material</note> in nature and known to pharmacists <note>original: *pharmacopolis*</note>; therefore, I hold that by the *Dudaim*, or Mandrakes, the **tincture** made from *magnesia saturnina* <note>original: *Magnesia saturnina*; an alchemical "magnesia" related to lead, often considered a primary starting material for the Philosopher's Stone</note> is to be understood.

This is also indicated by the Cabalistic root. At that same time, he also had the books written by the Egyptians on *alchemy*—specifically on making gold and silver—collected and burned, so that the Egyptians could not gather treasures from this art or rely on superfluous riches to rebel against the Romans in the future.

Dear reader, I cannot leave it unmentioned how, although the Patriarchs and Prophets, Moses and others, were **Adepts** <term>Adept: In the tradition of alchemy, an "Adept" is a master who has successfully achieved the "Great Work," specifically the creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</term>, they nevertheless never made use of this **Medicine** <note>original: "Medicinae"; likely referring to the Universal Medicine or the Elixir of Life derived from the Philosopher's Stone.</note> except upon prior divine command, just as you will gather from what follows.

reports Holy Scripture in the 26th verse: that God promised **Moses** that if he would walk in His way, he should be freed from all Egyptian diseases. For wood in the **vegetable kingdom** <note>original: "regno vegetabili"; one of the three traditional divisions of nature (animal, vegetable, mineral).</note> signifies growth; but the matter of metals promotes growth and fertility, therefore wood is taken to represent the Matter <note>The "Matter" refers to the Prima Materia, the starting base for the Philosopher's Stone.</note>. Furthermore, this **עץ** (*Etz*), that is wood, has in the "great number" 160, in the "middle" 16, and in the "small" 7 <note>This refers to Gematria, a system of Jewish numerology. The letter Ayin (ע) is 70 and Tsade (ץ) is 90, totaling 160. In "small" numbering, 1+6+0 = 7.</note>. Namely, through our Matter, God indicated to **Moses** how he should sweeten the waters; but because **Moses** knew how to prepare the **Medicine**, and also knew the **Matter** beforehand, yet wished to do nothing without a special divine command, he was sufficiently tested, so that he preferred to endure the speeches of the overflowing and grumbling people rather than seeking to make himself great through his art; therefore God granted him the blessing, as it were, that he, **Moses**, would be freed from all diseases, and would remain exempt from the necessity of using this **Medicine**.

## Ninth Chapter.

Solomon—to whom at his birth the name <term>Jadid-Jah</term> <note>Hebrew *Jedidiah* (יְדִידְיָהּ), meaning "Beloved of the Lord."</note> (that is, the Beloved of God) was given by God through the prophet Nathan, as can be seen in 2 Samuel 12:25—was, according to the testimony of Holy Scripture, the wisest of all men who were before him and who shall come after him. Thus, one would have to deny the entire Art <note>The "Art" refers to alchemy, the secret science of transmutation.</note> if one wished to say that he did not possess it; and consequently, there would have to have been wiser people in the world, which would be absurd to say against the tenor of Holy Scripture <note>original: "quod absurdum esset dicere contra tenorem sacrae scripturae"</note>, which cannot lie.

## Chapter Thirteen.

Job, who is both well-known and praised by all men—or, as he is better named according to the original text: איוב (*Jiob*) <note>The Hebrew name for Job</note>—emerges as one of the Adepts <note>In alchemical tradition, an "Adept" is someone who has mastered the secrets of nature and successfully created the Philosopher's Stone.</note> by virtue of his name, his historical description, the children he fathered, and otherwise his entire life’s path.

It is enough for me that no one can deny that the Book of *Job* is presented to us humans as a *moral* lesson, intended to instill patience within us. From this story now arise the following questions: 1) Why were the sons of *Jiob* never named, neither before his temptation nor afterward, while the daughters were specified by name? 2) The messenger announced to him the death of his sons through the collapse of the house; however, regarding the daughters (who, as seen in Holy Scripture, were guests at their eldest brother’s house), not a word is mentioned as to whether they died or remained alive. 3) *Jiob* received double of everything; yet it is not written that he had 14 [sons], but rather that in the end he had only 7 sons, just as he did not receive 6 daughters, but only 3, as in the beginning. <note>The author is questioning the "doubling" of Job's restoration; while his livestock was doubled in number, his children were replaced with the exact same number he had before.</note> 4) It does not say in the verse that his children were born to him at the last, but rather it is said: "And there were to him," etc. Now, if the sons were dead according to the messenger’s report of the house’s collapse, then their [new] birth would have to be reported. <note>The author suggests the phrasing "there were to him" (German: *es waren ihm*) instead of "there were born to him" might imply the original children survived.</note> 5) Why are the names of the daughters listed at the end and not at the beginning?

<meta>The author continues from the previous page's discussion of the order of the metallic kingdom, transitioning into a philosophical defense of alchemy based on Aristotelian principles of matter and form.</meta>

<page-num>85</page-num>
<sig>F 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: Because (Weil)</meta>

->*————*<-

In the *mineral kingdom* <note>original: *regno minerali*</note>, the *bodies* <note>original: *corpora*</note> are merely *simple mixtures* <note>original: *simpliciter mixta*</note> and consist *of similar parts* <note>original: *ex partibus similiaribus*</note>; therefore they *differ* only *according to their elemental mixture* <note>original: *secundum mixtionem elementarem*</note>. Such *mixtures* <note>original: *mixta*</note> now consist of the 4 elements, namely: Fire, Air, Water, and Earth; therefore they also contain 4 properties within themselves, as: heat, cold, moisture, and dryness. Aristotle maintains that in all three kingdoms <note>The animal, vegetable, and mineral kingdoms.</note>, there is one and the same *Prime Matter* <note>original: *Materia prima*. In ancient philosophy, this is the fundamental substance of the universe, which has no qualities of its own until it is "informed."</note>, and that bodies are distinguished only through their *substantial form* <note>original: *formam substantialem*. The essential nature or "blueprint" that makes a thing what it is.</note>; thus such *Prime Matter*, as soon as it is *informed* <note>original: *informiret*. Given shape or essence by a specific form.</note> by a form, is called *Secondary Matter* <note>original: *Materia secunda*</note>. For all *bodies* <note>original: *corpora*</note> or *physical substances* <note>original: *substantiae physicae*</note>—because they are composed *of Prime Matter and substantial form* <note>original: *ex Materia prima et forma substantiali*</note>, indeed the elements themselves, as they now *exist* in the *nature of things* <note>original: *rerum natura*</note> *outside of a mixture* <note>original: *extra mixtionem*</note>—are already *secondary matter*, because the *Aristotelian prime matter*, *without any substantial or accidental form* <note>original: *absque omni forma substantiali et accidentali*</note>, is considered according to the teaching of the *Peripatetics* <note>The followers of Aristotle's school of philosophy.</note> *through the abstraction of the mind* <note>original: *per abstractionem mentis*</note>, and is thus a pure *conceptual being* <note>original: *ens rationis*. A theoretical concept that exists in the mind rather than as a tangible physical object.</note>. Therefore, the *Hermetic Philosopher* <note>An alchemist.</note> does not take this *Aristotelian prime matter* into consideration, but rather remains solely with the *secondary matter*, and from it draws out his own *Prime Matter* through the Art <note>The "Art" is Alchemy itself.</note>. He considers this matter, however, in two ways, namely: *with respect to nature and to the art* <note>original: *respectu naturae et artis*</note>. *With respect to nature* <note>original: *Respectu naturae*</note>, he *distinguishes* <note>original: *distinguiret*</note> the *matter* into the *remote, intermediate, and proximate* <note>original: *in remotam, mediam et proximam*</note>.

<summary>This page explains the philosophical foundation of alchemy, distinguishing between the theoretical "Prime Matter" of Aristotle and the "Secondary Matter" which alchemists actually manipulate. It details how the four elements and their properties (heat, cold, moisture, dryness) constitute all physical mixtures in the mineral kingdom.</summary>

<keywords>Aristotle, prime matter, secondary matter, four elements, Peripatetics, Hermetic philosophy, substantial form, mineral kingdom, alchemy</keywords>

--- Page 88 ---
<meta>The author transitions from the general Aristotelian definitions of matter and form to a specific application in the mineral kingdom, arguing that all metals share a single underlying substance and differ only in their superficial qualities.</meta>

<page-num>86</page-num>
<header>-*- ════════ -*-</header>

Because my intention here is to treat minerals and metals, I will describe their *remote matter, intermediate matter,* and *proximate matter* <note>original: *materiam remotam, mediam und proximam*. Alchemists divided the developmental stages of a substance into these three categories to show how raw elements eventually become refined metal.</note>.

The first consists of the powers and properties of the elements, and of the **influences of the heavens and the stars** <note>original: *influentus coeli und astrorum*</note>, which are poured from the sky and the stars into the earth. When these penetrate into the veins of the earth, a viscous, slimy matter is formed from them, which is then called the **intermediate matter** <note>original: *media*</note>. This slimy essence—after circulating for a long time within the earth through rising and falling, being thinned by heat and thickened by cold—is brought through repeated **dissolution** and **coagulation** <note>original: *Resolution und Koagulation*. The fundamental alchemical process of breaking a substance down and solidifying it again.</note> into a metallic and mercurial nature. This is the **proximate matter of metals** <note>original: *materia proxima metallorum*</note> and is the same in all metals. Therefore, experience shows that metals mix with one another during melting; so much so that if one melts only a dram <note>original: *Quentchen*. A small historical unit of weight, roughly 3.6 grams.</note> of gold into a hundredweight <note>original: *Centner*</note> of lead, or vice versa, the assayer <note>original: *Probirer*</note> finds the added metal in every grain <note>original: *Gran*</note> of the mass. This is because their **proximate matter** <note>original: *materia proxima*</note> is of one and the same essence, and from that which becomes gold, the remaining metals are also generated; they differ only **accidentally** <note>original: *accidentaliter*. In Aristotelian philosophy, an "accident" is a property like color or hardness that can change without changing the essential nature of the thing.</note> and not **according to their species** <note>original: *quoad speciem*</note>. Thus, that supposed argument collapses, namely: **"One species cannot be changed into another"** <note>original: *Una species nequit mutari in aliam*. This was a common counter-argument against alchemy; the author is arguing that all metals are actually the same species, just in different stages of "perfection."</note>;

<meta>catchword: but (atqui)</meta>

<summary>The author explains the alchemical formation of metals from celestial influences into a "slimy" intermediate matter, eventually becoming a "proximate matter" that is shared by all metals. This provides the philosophical justification for transmutation, arguing that metals differ only in accidental qualities rather than their essential species.</summary>
<keywords>remote matter, intermediate matter, proximate matter, celestial influence, dissolution, coagulation, transmutation, Aristotelian physics, assaying</keywords>

<meta>The author continues the argument that all metals share a single essence, refuting the logical objection that metals are distinct species that cannot be transformed. He transitions from discussing the 'material cause' (the substance) to the 'efficient cause' (the forces of heat and cold) that shape minerals within the earth.</meta>

<page-num>87</page-num>

And yet, metals differ in species; therefore one cannot be changed into another <note>original: *atqui metalla specie differunt; ergo unum in aliud mutari nequit*. This is a common counter-argument the author is preparing to debunk.</note>. For the minor premise <note>The second part of a logical argument (syllogism) that applies a general rule to a specific case.</note> in this syllogism is false; because metals differ only <term>according to greater or lesser digestion or concoction [ripening]</term> <note>original: *secundum maiorem vel minorem digestionem aut concoctionem*. In alchemical theory, metals 'ripened' in the earth like fruit on a tree; gold was the final, 'cooked' stage of this process.</note>, and therefore only <term>differ accidentally</term> <note>original: *accidentaliter differiren*. In Aristotelian logic, an 'accident' is a property like color or hardness that can change without changing the fundamental nature of the thing.</note>. From this it is clearly to be concluded: that the <term>proximate matter [the immediate substance]</term> <note>original: *materia proxima*</note>, provided it is not prevented by the <term>accidental properties</term>, has the potentiality to become gold.

Enough concerning the <term>material cause with respect to Nature</term> <note>original: *caussa materiali respectu Naturae*</note>: much could also be reported concerning the matter <term>with respect to Art</term> <note>original: *respectu Artis*. Here 'Art' refers to Alchemy—the human skill used to complete or accelerate the work of nature.</note>; however, more of that can be gathered from what precedes and follows. Yet I will tell you the main point in two words: Where Nature has ceased to work in the Perfect and Imperfect, there Art must begin.

Now we will consider the <term>efficient cause</term> <note>original: *caussam efficientem*. The primary force or 'agent' that triggers a change.</note>, which consists of heat and cold. For heat thins the matter or vapors and moves them so that they rise and descend; then the pure vapors are fixed <note>original: *figiret*. To 'fix' meant to transform a volatile gas or liquid into a stable solid that can endure fire.</note> into gold and silver by the cold. But if they are impure, then—provided they penetrate into soft and porous earth—they are coagulated with their impurity into imperfect metals. But if they sink into hard rock: such as <term>quartz</term>, <term>hornstone</term> <note>A hard, flint-like mineral often found in ore veins.</note>, and the like, they become, because they

<summary>This page argues that metals are essentially the same and only differ in their 'ripeness' or external traits. It introduces the 'efficient cause' of metal-making: the movement of vapors through heat and their solidification through cold.</summary>

Every thing is recognized by its properties. Now we find various properties among the metals; specifically in gold, even if it is melted many times, we see that it nevertheless never decreases in weight, and is therefore <term>fire-resistant</term> <note>In alchemy, a substance is "fire-resistant" if it does not oxidize, evaporate, or turn to ash when heated.</note>. Silver is indeed also fire-resistant; however, it loses a very small amount of weight during melting. Copper and iron, on the other hand, are very <term>difficult to melt</term> <note>original: *hartflüssig*; referring to metals that require intense heat to liquefy or do not flow easily</note> and are unstable in the fire, so that as often as one melts them, they lose much weight, as much of them turns into <term>slag</term> <note>original: *Schlaken*; the stony waste matter separated from metals during the smelting process</note>. Tin and lead are <term>easy to melt</term> <note>original: *leichtflüssig*</note> and at the same time very unstable in the fire; the cause of which is: whereas gold carries nothing foreign or impure with it, its <term>proximate metallic matter</term> <note>original: *materia proxima metallica*; the immediate substance from which a metal is formed</note>—that is, the <term>radical moisture</term> <note>original: *humidum radicale*; the essential fluid principle in alchemy</note> and <term>radical dryness</term> <note>original: *siccum radicale*; the essential solid principle</note>—are so united in the smallest and most subtle <term>atoms</term> <note>original: *atomis*</note> or <term>minimal particles</term> <note>original: *particulis minimis*</note> that one can no longer be separated from the other. Consequently, the moisture preserves the dryness from combustibility, while the dryness prevents the moisture, so that it does not [evaporate] in the fire

<meta>This page continues the technical explanation of how the alchemist (the "artist") uses gold to create a catalyst for transmutation. It builds on the previous discussion of how the balance of moisture and dryness determines a metal's perfection.</meta>

...promoted [refining], thus **exalted** <note>In alchemy, to "exalt" a substance means to raise its potency, purity, or dignity to a higher level.</note>, so that through their heated quality they can provide a new **efficient cause** <note>original: *caussam efficientem*; the active agency that produces an effect.</note>. It works in this manner: When an imperfect body, afflicted with impurities, is melted in the fire or brought into flux, and a small particle of the prepared **Tincture** <note>Also known as the Philosopher's Stone, the Tincture is the concentrated substance used to transform base metals into gold.</note> is added to it, a new reaction arises from this **exalted moving cause** <note>original: *exaltata caussa mouente*</note>. This cause [strips away] from this imperfect body its **accidentally** <note>original: *accidentaliter*; in philosophy, "accidental" refers to non-essential qualities that are not part of the thing's true inner nature.</note> attached...

<meta>This page continues the technical explanation of how the "exalted" fire-power or Sulphur of gold acts upon imperfect metals to complete their transformation. It links alchemical theory to Aristotelian physics regarding the nature of heat.</meta>

<page-num>91</page-num>

...separated from the impurities, and grasps the **proximate matter** <note>original: *materiam proximam*; the immediate substance from which something is formed.</note> within it, mixes itself **intimately** <note>original: *intime*</note> with the same, awakens its potential fire-power, and transforms it into gold.

According to the Aristotelian principle: *Fire or heat gathers homogeneous things and scatters heterogeneous things* <note>original Latin: *Ignis vel calor congregat homogenea, et disgregat heterogenea*</note>; that is, by fire <term>Δ</term> <note>The alchemical symbol for fire.</note> or heat, the **homogeneous**—or essentially similar things—are gathered together, and the **heterogeneous**—that is, the opposing things—are parted and separated.

Since the **physical tincture** <term>Tinctura physica</term> <note>A potent alchemical substance, often synonymous with the Philosopher's Stone, used to "stain" or transform base metals into gold.</note> contains within itself a 100-, 1,000-, indeed a 10,000-fold fire **with respect to its decoction and repeated multiplication** <note>original Latin: *respectu decretionis et reiteratae multiplicationis*; refers to the process of increasing the Tincture's potency through repeated boiling and refining.</note>; it is therefore easy to understand that when it is applied to the imperfect metals (just as happens **in projection** <note>original: *in proiectione*; the final stage of the Great Work, where the Tincture is cast onto molten base metal to trigger transmutation.</note>), it gathers the **homogeneous** things—that is, the mercurial parts—and intimately mixes and unites with them; but on the other hand, it separates the **heterogeneous** things—that is, the **dregs, slag, and impurities** <note>original Latin: *feces, scorias et impuritates*</note>—from them.

However, it should not be understood here that the entire imperfect metal is **transmuted** into gold, but only as much as it contains of the **proximate matter** by weight; for the impurity becomes slag. Therefore, for **projection**, the artists <note>A traditional term for practicing alchemists.</note> have commonly taken those bodies which carry the most **proximate matter** with them, such as tin, lead, and **Mercury** <term>☿</term> <note>The alchemical symbol for Mercury or quicksilver, viewed as the "mother" of all metals.</note>; but rarely cop- <note>The text cuts off here; likely "copper" (Kupfer).</note>

<summary>This page explains the mechanism of alchemical transmutation based on the Aristotelian principle that heat unites similar substances and expels foreign ones. It clarifies that only the pure 'proximate matter' within a base metal is turned into gold, while the impurities are cast off as slag.</summary>

And let this be enough said regarding the possibility of the **transmutation** <term>Transmutation</term> <note>The alchemical process of changing one substance, specifically a base metal, into another, such as gold or silver.</note> of metals, *both against those who deny the Art, and those who boast of it in vain* <note>original Latin: *tam contra Artem negantes, quam de ea vane sese iactantes*</note>. I therefore take occasion from this to believe, and to arrive at the thought, that the **juice of the earth** <term>succus terrae</term> <note>The vital fluid or mineral-rich moisture found within the soil, thought by alchemists to be the life-blood of the world.</note> is the **feminine seed** <term>sperma foemininum</term>, or the female seed of plants or growths in the **vegetable kingdom** <note>original: *vegetabilischen Reiche*; the realm of plant life.</note>. We see, therefore, that in this kingdom also, the male and female are ordained for **generation** <term>Generation</term> <note>The biological or alchemical process of reproduction and growth.</note>, and that the earth—namely its juice—provides the feminine element. Whether this "wife" [the earth] has two husbands—namely, a vegetable and a mineral one—is what I shall further discuss. That is to say: just as this juice of the earth unlocks, nourishes, and increases the vegetable seed—that is, its vegetable husband or **masculine seed** <term>Semen masculinum</term>—whether it is likewise capable, as a **feminine seed** <term>semen femininum</term> or **radical solvent** <term>menstruum radicale</term> <note>original Latin: *menstruum radicale*. ...finally, a <term>ferment</term><note>original: *Ferment*; in alchemy, a substance (often gold or silver) added to the "Stone" to give it the specific quality of that metal, allowing it to then transmute other base metals into gold or silver.</note>, namely: gold, is added to it, a point upon which all philosophers agree. Now, if the <term>Mercury</term><note>original: *Mercurius*; not common quicksilver, but the "Mercury of the Philosophers," a specialized substance believed to be the life-force of metals.</note> were not <term>homogeneous</term><note>original: *homogenisch*; of the same kind or nature.</note> with the gold, then something foreign would indeed be present.

The ferment, or gold, is a metal, or of a metallic nature, as is the <term>Tincture</term><note>original: *Tinktur*; the concentrated alchemical medicine or "Stone" capable of transforming metals.</note> or the <term>Stone</term><note>original: *Lapis*; the Philosopher's Stone.</note>; because the metals are intended to be changed or improved by it, it must therefore be of a metallic nature. Consequently, all plant and animal substances are of a contrary nature and are foreign things <term>with respect to the ferment and the tincture</term><note>original Latin: *respectu fermenti et tincturae*.</note>. Therefore, if the Mercury of the Philosophers—acting as the <term>radical solvent</term><note>original Latin: *Solvens radicale*; a solvent that dissolves a substance down to its very "root" or essence without destroying its core nature.</note>—is to dissolve the gold from its root, or if your tincture (which you have prepared according to your whim out of some adventurous matter) is to mix or unite itself <term>most intimately</term><note>original Latin: *per intima*; literally "through the innermost parts."</note> with the gold as its ferment, it is necessary that the Mercury of the Philosophers and the matter from which it is extracted be homogeneous, or of that same—that is, metallic—nature; as all philosophers confirm.

But I know what is in your head: you find in *Homer* the herb <term>Moly</term><note>In the *Odyssey*, a magical herb given by Hermes to Odysseus to protect him against Circe’s magic.</note> (which, however, is better named <term>Molybdenum</term><note>original: *Molybdena*; here the author is likely connecting the mythical herb to lead-like minerals, as the Greek *molybdos* means lead.</note>); know, however, that this is only cited as an allegory. <meta>This page continues the author's critique of alchemists who seek the "Matter" of the Great Work in common substances. It follows the catchword "da" (since) from the previous page, where the author began explaining why certain practitioners fail.</meta>

since they indeed want to force their Matter out of common sulfur, from vitriol, and similar **lesser minerals** <term>original Latin: *mineralibus minoribus*; refers to secondary minerals like salts or sulfates, which alchemists considered less "mature" than metallic ores</term>; but these people should only consider how the aforementioned things are generated **on the surface of the earth** <term>original Latin: *in superficie terrae*</term>; then they would quite easily recognize whether such things can be useful for their undertaking or not? Far be it from me, my dear reader! to describe any specific recipe or process; rather, my intention is solely to lead you away from wrong paths and toward the right way. Therefore, pay close attention! For here one finds many errors which nevertheless appear similar to the truth; do not let yourself be seduced by them.

The **lesser minerals** <term>original Latin: *Mineralia minora*</term> have nothing in themselves, therefore they can impart nothing to others. They consist mostly of an **acid** <term>original Latin: *acido*</term> and an earthly, alkaline, or metallic **base** <term>original Latin: *Basi*</term>, which frequently arises from mineral vapors and their repercussion <note>In early chemistry, "repercussion" referred to the cooling and condensation or "striking back" of rising vapors into solid form.</note>. If these vapors find an **acid**, they coagulate with the **acid** and finally become either a **sulfur** <term>original Latin: *sulphur*</term>, vitriol, or **alum** <term>original Latin: *Alaun*</term>, according to the strength of the **acid** and the nature of the **base**, as is demonstrated in **experimental chemistry** <term>original Latin: *Chemia experimentali*</term>. For the **acid** is nearly the same in everything. You can easily find the proof of this: take **sulfur**, and make from it an **oil by the bell** <term>original Latin: *Oleum per campanam*; a method of producing sulfuric acid by burning sulfur under a glass bell jar and collecting the condensed moisture</term>; place iron or copper into this oil to **dissolve** <note>original: *solviren*.</note>; then you can have **Vitriol** <note>The text likely continues onto the next page as "Vitriol of Mars" (*Vitriolum Martis*), which is iron sulfate.</note>

<meta>This page continues the author's argument from page 104, completing the sentence regarding experiments of iron (Mars) and copper (Venus). The author is explaining why "lesser minerals" are insufficient for the Great Work, despite their chemical similarities to metals.</meta>

<page-num>105</page-num>

[experiments] of **Mars** <term>original Latin: *Martis*; refers to iron</term> or **Venus** <term>original Latin: *Veneris*; refers to copper</term>, and have thus converted **sulfur** into **vitriol** <term>a sulfate mineral</term>. If you use the **oil of sulfur** <term>original Latin: *Oleum sulphuris*; concentrated sulfuric acid produced by burning sulfur</term> by itself, you will find it has the very same **virtue** <note>In early modern science, "virtue" refers to the inherent power, property, or medicinal efficacy of a substance.</note> as the **oil of vitriol** <term>original Latin: *Oleum Vitrioli*; sulfuric acid produced from vitriol salts</term>; and this is proof enough.

Furthermore: that vitriol, or sulfur—which are almost one and the same—arise from the **repercussion** <note>In early geology and alchemy, "repercussion" referred to the process where rising subterranean vapors hit the cooler crust of the earth and "rebounded" or condensed into solid minerals.</note> of the earth’s vapors; of this you can make a test in the following way: you will find indeed a blue, a green, and a white vitriol; the blue is **veneric** <term>copper-based</term>, the green is **martial** <term>iron-based</term>, but the white is **zinc-like**. Consequently, because it is otherwise well known that the metals are buried much deeper in the earth than the **lesser minerals**; it must follow of necessity that this small amount of metal, which they share in, arises from the rebounding or **repercussion** of metallic vapors; for otherwise I would not know where the metallic essence should come from.

Now the question is, which part is supposed to be good? The **acid** <term>original Latin: *acidum*</term> is a **corrosive** <term>original Latin: *corrosivum*</term>, which is of no use in the [Alchemical] Work, but rather does much harm. The metallic part is copper, iron, or zinc; which could not even cure itself, much less impart anything to another. Besides, it cannot be denied that the **oil of vitriol** performs very good service in the **anatomy of metals** <term>original Latin: *anatomia metallorum*; the chemical analysis or breaking down of metallic substances into their components</term>, just as the *Alchemy Unveiled* <note>original Latin title: *Alchemia denudata*; a well-known alchemical compendium of the era</note> reports. To this...

<meta>This page continues the author's critique of "lesser minerals" (mineralia minora) like sulfur and vitriol. While the previous page discussed the chemical relationship between iron, copper, and acids, this page transitions to a more allegorical and specific discussion of mineral ores, warning the seeker not to be distracted by flashy but useless materials.</meta>

<page-num>106</page-num>

For the **Great Work** <note>The *Magnum Opus*, or the process of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, however, I warn you not to choose any of these aforementioned **lesser minerals** <term>original Latin: *mineralibus minoribus*; refers to substances like sulfur, salts, and vitriols which were considered "incomplete" compared to metals</term> as your starting material.

I will descend one step deeper into the earth. There I encounter the one whom my soul loves, though she is also the most despised. Her companions are preferred over her by all those who search among minerals. But I assure you that if they knew you **intrinsically** <note>original Latin: *intrinsece*; meaning "inwardly" or according to your true essence.</note>, they would abandon **red silver ore**, **red gold-silver ore**, **Saxon bismuth**, **Nagybánya silver and gold ore** <note>Nagybánya (modern-day Baia Mare, Romania) was a famous mining center in the Habsburg Empire.</note>, **gold concentrate** <term>original German: *Goldschlich*; finely crushed ore or gold-bearing slime</term>, **silver glance** <term>original: *Glaserz*; argentite</term>, **native cinnabar** <term>original Latin: *Cinnabarim nativam*; the primary ore of mercury</term>, fine **gold and silver pyrites**, as well as various **quartzes** and other such things.

Now, what do those who search in these minerals desire? I have heard it many times with my own ears that they seek only a **raw and immature sulfur of the sun** <term>original Latin: *sulphur solis*; a metaphorical "spiritual" sulfur associated with gold</term>, which they want to purify and bring to a **tincture** <term>the Elixir or agent of transmutation</term>. This intention sounds good, beautiful, and pleasant; however, such a mineral is not to be found. 

Many a **dealer in chemicals** <term>original: *Materialist*; in this period, a merchant who traded in raw drugs, chemicals, and minerals</term>, or a similar **shop-hand** <term>original: *Gewölb-bedienter*; literally "vault-servant," referring to an apothecary's or merchant's assistant</term>, when he sees a mineral that glitters a little, says: "Oh! If so-and-so were alive, or present here, he would surely gladly pay one **thaler** or a **ducat** for one pound of this ore." But I say to you: I would not give a single **penny** <term>original: *Groschen*; a low-value silver coin</term> for it for the sake of the work.

<meta>This page continues the author's critique of seeking the "philosopher's stone" within complex ores. While the previous page listed various precious minerals like red silver ore and cinnabar, this page argues that these materials are deceptive and dangerous due to their arsenic content, and that they only yield "ordinary" gold or silver anyway.</meta>

<page-num>107</page-num>

Just examine your best material for a moment; ask any mining expert whether **red silver ore** <note>original: *Rothgüldenerz*; a silver-antimony or silver-arsenic mineral.</note> is not almost pure silver, and **red gold-silver ore** almost pure gold? The **arsenic** present there has turned it into a **glass** <note>original: *Vitrum*; refers to the glassy, crystalline appearance of these ores.</note>, and you can easily make this yourself, though it is without benefit and only causes harm. For it is well known that arsenic "robs" <note>In historical metallurgy, arsenic was said to "rob" or "steal" because it caused precious metals to vaporize or become brittle and lost during the smelting process.</note>:

Now tell me, which part of this material do you desire? As for the **arsenic**, it is surely known to you that it is unfit for the **Great Work** <note>original: *Hauptarbeit*; the primary alchemical process of transmutation.</note>; indeed, everyone who loves their life should be warned against it. Consequently, you will necessarily seek the gold or silver from this material; which you will indeed find, but what kind? Certainly none other than the **ordinary** kind. What can you hope for here, since you do not intend to work through the **anatomy of metals** <term>original: *Anatomiam metallorum*; a chemical process of breaking a metal down into its fundamental "principles" of salt, sulfur, and mercury</term>? Even if you did, the ordinary metal would be much more useful to you, as no arsenic is present in it.

Secondly, I ask you, since you seek a **sulfur of the sun or of the moon** <term>original Latin: *sulphur solis vel Lunae*; alchemical terms for the "essential soul" or active principle extracted from gold (sun) or silver (moon)</term>, how do you know that such a thing is to be found in this or that **mineral**? Perhaps none might be inside. If you say that you find gold in it when you smelt it, I reply to you that such gold is not immature, but has reached **perfection**...

...matured. A mother gave birth to seven sons and one daughter; among whom three—namely two sons and the one daughter—came into the world at once.
