# First Chapter.

First, with God’s help, I will begin to teach how one should prepare, purify, sublime, and coagulate the philosophical mercury for the blessed Stone <note>The "Philosopher's Stone," the legendary substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold and grant long life</note>, making it beautifully white, like a crystal. Take 2 pounds of vitriol <term>Vitriol: a sulfate mineral, usually of iron or copper, used as a powerful corrosive</term>, 1 pound of dried <term>common salt (sal commune)</term>, let it dissolve in a bowl, and when it has dissolved and begins to thicken again, have <term>Hungarian quicksilver (mercurium vivum Hungaricum)</term> <note>Mercury sourced from Hungarian mines was particularly valued by alchemists for its supposed purity</note> at hand, and squeeze it through a leather cloth into the vitriol and salt. Stir it well together with a wooden spatula over a gentle heat so that it becomes completely dry; then grind it well on a rubbing stone until no more "living" <note>liquid or metallic</note> mercury can be seen. Should any living mercury still be visible, moisten it with vinegar and set it back on gentle heat to dry, and stir it with the spatula; and when it is dry, grind it well again on the rubbing stone so that the mercury disappears entirely <note>The goal here is to "kill" the mercury, or chemically bond it with the salts so it is no longer a liquid metal</note>. Afterward, put it all together into a <term>sublimation glass (sublimirglas)</term> <note>A specialized glass vessel used for heating solids until they vaporize and reform as a purified solid at the top of the container</note>, well-sealed with luting <note>original: *verlutirt*; "luting" was a clay or paste used to make laboratory apparatus airtight</note>,

<summary>This page provides the first practical steps for creating the Philosopher's Stone, detailing the process of "killing" mercury by grinding it with vitriol and common salt. This preparation prepares the substance for the next stage of purification through sublimation.</summary>

<keywords>alchemy, mercury, vitriol, common salt, sublimation, purification, laboratory practice</keywords>

--- Page 120 ---
<meta>The previous page concluded with instructions to place the mixture of vitriol, salt, and mercury into a sublimation glass. This page continues the specific heating instructions to complete the purification process.</meta>

<image-desc>A decorative horizontal divider ornament consisting of a central bar with stylized star or floral motifs at each end.</image-desc>

-pared, <note>The text begins mid-word from the previous page's catchword "tirt," likely completing "praeparirt" (prepared)</note> let the lute <term>Lutum: a clay or cement paste used to seal alchemical vessels airtight</term> dry, and place it in a sublimation fire <note>Heat specifically regulated to cause a solid to vaporize and then solidify again in a purified form</note>, though not deeper into the fire than the height of the matter inside the vessel. Sublime it first with a gentle fire for four or five hours, so that the moisture leaves it completely. A helm <term>Helm: the cap or head of a still, also known as an alembic</term> should be placed atop the vessel, attached to a receiver <term>Recipiente: a vessel designed to collect distilled or sublimated substances</term>, so that one may see the moisture. 

When the moisture has passed over, increase the fire for four to five hours until the vessel finally glows; then let the fire go out and allow everything to cool down thoroughly. Afterward, open the vessel and take out the mercury, which will be completely gray. Then one must again take 2 pounds of vitriol and 1 pound of salt and proceed with them as was done the first time; and this must be performed four times. 

After the fourth sublimation, however, grind the mercury and the residue <note>original: "feces"</note> together and sublime it; thus the mercury will have become 2 lots <term>Lot: a historical unit of weight, roughly 1/32nd of a pound or about 15 grams</term> heavier from the sulfur <note>original: "sulphur"</note> of the vitriol, and it will be as beautiful, bright, and clear as a crystal. After that, sublime this mercury two or three more times by itself <note>original: "per se"</note>; then it is ready for the Work and awaits its soul <note>original: "animam"</note> or sulfur <note>original: "Sulphur"</note> <note>In alchemy, the "soul" or "sulfur" represents the active, combustible, and masculine principle that must be reunited with the purified "mercury" or feminine principle</note>. But preserve this mercury well.

## Second Chapter.

Now, with the help of God, I will further teach—

<meta>This page continues the metallurgical and alchemical process described on the previous page. The text picks up from the catchword "and" (und), completing the phrase "and to explain" (und zu erklären).</meta>

...and explain how the *ferment of the sun* <note>original: "fermentum solis"</note> or the *sulfur of the sun* <note>original: "sulphuris solis"</note> is prepared and should be brought into the *first essence* <note>original: "primum ens"</note>, so that one may reach the *solution* <note>original: "Solution"</note>, and such a ferment may be crucified with its prepared spirit and rise again. <note>The terms "crucified" and "rise again" are alchemical metaphors for the breakdown of a substance and its subsequent purification or "resurrection" into a higher state.</note>

### *Purification of the Sun.* <note>original: "Purgatio solis"; here "Sun" refers to gold, the solar metal.</note>

Take one part of fine gold and pour it through three parts of *Hungarian antimony* <note>original: "antimonii Hungarici"</note>; but this pouring should occur four times. Each time, one must let the regulus <term>König (regulus): the metallic button or "king" found at the bottom of a crucible after smelting</term> flow with three parts of *antimony* and pour it to the regulus; that is to say, purified through the *stibium* <note>another name for antimony</note>. After the fourth pouring and melting, pour all the reguli together and melt and pour them with three times their weight of *tartar* <note>original: "tartaro"</note>, so that the *antimony* for the most part evaporates; then place the *antimony* with the gold upon a cupel <term>Treibscherben: a shallow, porous vessel used in refining precious metals</term> and give it a strong charcoal fire, so that the *antimony* burns away from the gold, and the gold becomes completely pure and fine. In this way, it reaches a high grade and far surpasses other gold. This gold must be beaten as thin as possible and brought to *calcination* <note>original: "Calcination"; the process of heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder or change its state.</note>.

Now make a *strong water* <note>original: "aquam fortem"; usually referring to nitric acid</note>. Take pure, refined *saltpeter* <note>original: "Niter"</note> and *vitriol*, as much of one as of the other; from these, distill a water with all its *spirits* <note>original: "Spiritibus"</note>. Of this *strong water* <note>original: "Aquafort"</note>, take one pound

### Calcination of the Sun <note>original: "Calcinatio Solis"; the process of reducing gold (the "Sun") into a fine powder or ash to make it chemically reactive.</note>.

Take your thinly beaten gold and cut it into the smallest pieces, and put them into a separating flask <note>original: "Scheidekolben"; a glass vessel with a long neck used in distillation and metal separation.</note>, and pour three parts of the water <note>meaning the aqua fortis previously prepared.</note> upon it. Then place it on gentle heat in an ash bath <note>original: "Aschenkapelle"; a container filled with sand or ash used to provide even, indirect heat to a glass vessel.</note> to dissolve, and if it does not all dissolve in the water, let it stand on the heat for one night. Afterward, place it in a water bath <note>original: "balneum Mariae"; literally "Mary’s bath," a double-boiler setup used for gentle, consistent heating.</note> and distill the <term>phlegma</term> <note>original: "Phlegma"; in alchemy, the watery, tasteless, and inactive portion of a liquid that rises first during distillation.</note> from it, down to the <term>spirits</term> <note>original: "spiritus"; the volatile, active essences of the substance.</note>, and let it cool well.

Now take the flask with the <term>gold solution</term> <note>original: "Goldsolution"; the liquid resulting from gold being dissolved in acid.</note> and place it in a little cold water; then pour just as much <term>spirit of wine</term> <note>original: "spiritum vini"; concentrated alcohol or ethanol.</note> into it as there was *phlegma* distilled off, adding it always a little at a time. When you have added all the spirit of wine, let it stand for a day and a night in cold water; seal <note>original: "sigillire"; to hermetically close the vessel, often with wax or "lute" (a clay paste), to prevent the escape of vapors.</note> it well at the top, and the next day place it on gentle heat and let it stand for three weeks. Then place it in the water bath and begin to distill, and thus distill it nine times by <term>cohobation</term> <note>original: "cohobationem"; the repeated process of pouring a distilled liquid back onto its residue and re-distilling it to concentrate its "virtue" or power.</note>.

In the final distillation, it must be distilled from the ash until it reaches dryness;

So that a complete union may now occur through **fixation** <note>the process of making a volatile substance stable under heat</note> by God’s grace, take in the name of God 6 parts of the prepared **spirit of mercury** and 4 parts of the prepared **ferment of the sun**, as the **soul** <note>original: *anima*</note>. ...sition. <note>completing the word "composition" from the previous page</note> These two parts should be well-ground together on a **grinding stone** <term>Reibestein; a flat stone, usually porphyry, used with a muller to grind and mix chemicals</term>, then placed in a **glass flask** <term>Kolbenglas; a round-bottomed glass vessel used for boiling and distillation</term> that is suitable for the purpose. The bottom of the flask must be coated with a good **lute** <note>original: *luto*; a protective paste made of clay, dung, or glass to protect the vessel from direct fire</note>, and an **alembic head** <term>Helm; the "helmet" or top part of a still where vapors collect</term> placed upon it. A **receiving vessel** <term>Recipient; the flask that collects the distilled substance</term> must be very well **sealed** <note>original: *lutirt*; joined using alchemical lute</note> to it, so that the **spirits** <note>original: *Geister*; here meaning volatile, potentially toxic vapors or gases</note> do not harm you, for they are quite dangerous.

Place it in a **heating chamber** <note>original: *Capelle*; refers here to the outer container of a sand-bath or furnace</note> with half sand and half ash, which has been very finely sifted through a **hair sieve**. The heating chamber must also be well secured so that no "side-heat" can escape from the sides; rather, the heat must all remain at the bottom. However, it must have its four **vent holes** so that the coals can burn.

Do not set the flask deeper into the ash than it is coated with the protective lute, and also no deeper into the sand than the level of the mass inside the glass. Begin to **sublimate** <note>original: *sublimiren*; to heat a solid until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again in the upper part of the vessel</note>, first with a gentle fire for five or six hours; then strengthen the fire for four or five hours until the ash or sand glows. Then cease the heat and let it cool by itself.

## Chapter Five.

### *The Sublimation of Gold and Mercury.* <note>original: *Sublimatio solis et mercurii*</note>

When it has become cold, take out what has risen <note>the sublimated material collected in the neck or head of the vessel</note> and weigh what remains at the bottom. For every four parts of the remainder, take six parts of **sublimated mercury** <note>original: *mercurii sublimati*; likely mercuric chloride, a common but highly toxic alchemical reagent</note>, prepared as described above. Grind these well together, including the material that had risen, and mix them thoroughly.

This sublimation must be repeated as often as necessary until everything that is ferment rises with its spirit and **mercury** <note>original: *mercurio*</note> and becomes spiritual. That which has risen must always be ground with that which remained below and then sublimated again. 

When everything has now been made volatile, sublimate it four more times together as before, so that it becomes truly spiritual and **subtle** <note>refined to its most pure and potent form</note>. After this, take it and grind it very finely and put it all together into a **vial** <term>Phiole; a small glass vessel</term> that is suitable, ensuring it is no more than half full and also no more than half-coated <note>likely referring to the protective coating of lute on the glass to withstand heat</note>. **Seal** <note>original: *sigillire*; to hermetically seal the glass, often by melting the neck shut</note> it well at the top, and place it thus

...into a **sand or ash bath** <note>original: *Sand- oder Aschencapelle*; a container filled with sand or ash used to provide even, indirect heat to a glass vessel</note>, and cover the **vial** <term>Phiole; a glass flask used in chemical operations</term> up to the halfway point. Begin to **sublimate** <term>sublimiren; the process of heating a substance until it vaporizes and then collects as a solid on a cooler surface, used for purification</term> with a gentle fire, and regulate your fire from hour to hour, making it stronger and stronger, until the matter rises into the neck of the **vial**. Then keep the fire always at the same degree, and no stronger, and let it stand thus for eight days in the **sublimation fire** <term>Sublimirfeuer; a steady heat maintained specifically to keep the substance in a state of vaporous transition</term>. After the eight days, let it cool and break it open; a beautiful, transparent matter will have **sublimated upward** <term>aufsublimiret</term>. 

Whatever remains at the bottom of the **vial**, throw that away; but carefully preserve that which has risen, for that is the spirit along with its soul. For this, praise God and pray that He gives His blessing to **proceed** <term>procediren; to move forward with the steps of the Great Work</term> further and to **fix** <term>figiren; "fixation," the process of making a volatile or liquid substance solid and resistant to heat</term> the matter. 

Thus you have the first and true **philosophical solution** <term>philosophische Solution; the "opening" or dissolving of a solid body into its essential, primordial state</term> and the dissolution of the body into its **first essence** <term>primum ens; the original, most basic state of a substance before it takes a specific form</term>, as such is described by **Geber** <note>Geber is the Latinized name of Jabir ibn Hayyan (c. 721–815), a legendary Islamic polymath and alchemist whose works formed the foundation of European alchemy.</note> and other philosophers, though covered with **dark spectacles** <note>original: *finstern Brille*; a metaphor for obscure, coded, or allegorical language meant to hide the truth from the uninitiated</note>. Therefore, we should also keep this secret, so that it is not made common and pearls are not cast before swine—especially to reveal it to no single selfish or stingy dog, lest one bring God's punishment upon oneself and thus be eternally damned, from which may God graciously preserve us.

...to proceed <note>completing the word from the previous page: likely "further" or "to advance"</note> in fixing this high work, so that one may come ever closer to the **Philosopher's Stone** <note>original: *Stein der Weisen*; the legendary substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold and granting eternal life</note>. For if one wishes to proceed correctly and reach the highest degree of perfection, one must possess a great deal of the **spirit of mercury** <note>original: *Geistes mercurii*; in alchemy, mercury represents the volatile, fluid principle of matter</note>, which has been prepared and set aside with its **ferment** <term>Ferment; a substance added to a base to "leaven" it and trigger a chemical or spiritual transformation</term>. If one repeats this often, one can benefit from it many thousandfold, for the greatest power lies hidden within it. 

Now take the prepared **spirit of mercury** with its **soul** <gloss>anima</gloss>—of which you must have a large amount—and divide it into nine equal parts. Take one of these parts and place it into a **fixing glass** <term>Figirglas; a specialized glass vessel designed to withstand the heat needed to "fix" or solidify a volatile substance</term>, and place it in a **sand bath** <note>original: *Sandcapelle*; a container of sand used to distribute heat evenly around the glass</note> to be fixed. Keep the other eight parts safe and pay close attention only to the fixing glass. 

When the matter **sublimates upward** <term>aufsublimirt; the process where a solid turns into vapor and reforms as a solid higher up in the vessel</term>, turn the fixing glass over and maintain it thus in a moderate fire until the third week. By then, the matter will no longer sublimate upward but will remain at the bottom and become **fixed** <term>fix; "fixation" is the alchemical goal of making a substance resistant to fire so that it no longer evaporates</term>. 

Continue now with a steady fire for another eight days, and when you see nothing more rising, let the fire go out and let everything become cold. Then, break open the fixing glass and take the fixed matter and rub it together well with another part of the reserved eight parts of **mercury**. Place it back into the **sublimation glass** <term>Sublimirglas</term> to sublimate, until the **fixed** rises again with the **unfixed** and becomes **volatile** <term>flüchtig; able to be vaporized</term>. However, if the parts of the unfixed matter are too few to make the fixed matter volatile, then add more...

## Chapter Eleven.

Now I will teach you **projection** <term>projection; the final stage of the Great Work, where a tiny amount of the alchemical medicine is "cast" onto molten base metal to transform it into gold</term>: first take one hundred parts of the purest gold, and mix into it one part of your **elixir**, which has been purified in the best way; put them together into a good **crucible** <term>crucible; a ceramic or graphite container used for melting substances at very high temperatures</term> that can withstand the fire, and place it in a **wind furnace** <term>wind furnace; a furnace designed to reach high heat by utilizing a natural or forced draft of air</term> for three days and nights, and let it flow in this manner. Then let it cool and break open the vessel; thus your gold is purely living gold and a medicine for all metals to transform them into good gold.

With every **imbibition** <note>literally "soaking" or "drinking"; the process of adding liquid to the solid Stone to increase its potency</note> you may take ten parts more gold—which is well worth noting—and it **tinges** <note>original: *tingiret*; in alchemy, to "tinge" is to transform the inner nature of a metal by "coloring" it with the properties of gold or silver</note> every time ten parts, then a hundred parts, and finally a thousand parts, then ten thousand parts, and so on in orderly number. And when one metal is tinged, that in turn tinges another.

Keep your eyes and ears diligently on the work, its articles, and points. Everything will teach itself through the work; indeed, a certain technique or insight may occur to one by chance if one pays diligent attention, so that the work might be brought to its end four, six, or even ten weeks sooner, which happened to me recently in this work. But do not despise the blessing and the help of Almighty God in this work; pray and work diligently and incessantly.

## Chapter Twelve.

Now, with divine help, I will teach you to make and prepare for the Work the *spirit of life* <note>original: *spiritum vitae*</note>, which is the *fifth essence* <term>fifth essence; also called the quintessence, this is the purest, most concentrated medicinal force extracted from a substance</term> in the Art and our *Azoth* <term>Azoth; a term for the universal solvent or the "mercury of the philosophers," believed to be the essential agent of transformation</term>, which wanders through the whole world. For within it lies the highest *secret* <note>original: *Secretum*</note> of the entire Work and mastery, and without it, nothing perfect is made in *medicine* <note>in this context, "medicine" refers to the alchemical elixir or the Philosopher's Stone itself</note> or born philosophically in our Art (although a dry path is also known, using only our *mercury* <note>original: *mercurio*</note> on metals *with the sun from sulfur* <note>original: *cum sole ex sulphure*; a reference to a process using gold and sulfur</note>, which is not at all lengthy). From this comes the Stone of the wise masters, which God has given to us freely.

Take the very best wine that can be obtained and distill its *spirit* <note>original: *spiritum*; the concentrated alcohol or "essence" of the wine</note> from it; collect this together, for you must have a great deal of it. You must rectify <term>rectify; to purify a liquid, such as alcohol, by distilling it repeatedly to remove impurities or water</term> it so often until it no longer has any *phlegm* <term>phlegm; the watery, inactive residue left over after the "spirit" or alcohol has been distilled away</term>.

**Note Well.** But as for the *phlegm* <note>original: *Phlegma*</note> that was drawn off from the *spirit* <note>original: *spiritu*</note>, one must draw off more than half of it and place it in the cellar among the *dregs* <note>original: *feces*; the solid, brown sediment remaining in the vessel after distillation</note>, which are brown, and let it stand there. Then little stones will crystallize <note>original: *schiessen*, literally "shoot" like crystals forming in a solution</note> within it; for from this is made the Stone, which

## Chapter Thirteen.

That such is true and that this philosophical process is correct, I can testify before God and the Truth: for I have worked it with my own hands twice from the ground up, and it is rightly to be called and esteemed as a high, noble treasure, which I would not even give up for the kingdom of the King of Spain. I also thank God, the Most High, that He has let me come so worthily to it, and even to the original version of it, just as it was sent from the Venetian lords' chemical palace <note>original: *Palatio Chymiatrico*; likely a reference to a state-controlled laboratory or workshop in Venice</note> by a fifty-two-year-old man—who had been kept within it against his will for his entire life—to his brother through a cunning plan born of brotherly love, who then passed it on to me.

...wise men. Along with me, many thousands of people in poverty have rejoiced in this, and have yet to rejoice; and from this same source, the following **Potable Gold** <term>Potable Gold; original: *Aurum potabile*, a legendary medicinal liquid made from gold, believed by alchemists to have universal healing powers and to grant longevity</term> has also emerged, which is not to be disregarded.

### *Potable Gold.*

Take gold and silver made through mercury <note>original: *Merkur*</note>, calcine <term>calcine; to heat a substance to high temperatures in order to reduce it to a fine powder or "ash"</term> it in a reverberatory furnace <term>reverberatory furnace; an oven where the flame and heat are deflected or "reverberated" downward onto the material</term> until it becomes like a swan <note>the "swan" is an alchemical symbol for the *albedo*, a stage where the matter becomes white and pure</note>. Then place the mercury into a cucurbit <term>cucurbit; a gourd-shaped glass vessel used as the base of a distillation apparatus</term> and pour onto it **vegetable philosophical water** <note>original: *aquam philos. vegetabilem*; likely a highly rectified spirit of wine or a specialized solvent</note>, and seal it well with lute <note>original: *verlutire*; to seal a glass vessel airtight using a paste of clay, lime, or dung</note>; then place it in a digestion <term>digestion; the process of applying steady, gentle heat over a long period</term> in warm ashes until the water turns red. Pour this off, and add more water until it no longer takes on color. Then take the lime <note>the "lime" or *calx* refers to the powdered remains of the metals</note> and extract its color once more as before.

Next, combine all the colored waters together and draw off the phlegm <term>phlegm; the watery, tasteless, and inactive part of a substance that remains or comes over during distillation</term> **by means of an alembic** <note>original: *per alembicum*; an alembic is the "cap" or hood of a still</note>. A red powder of gold and silver will remain, though it will appear greenish-brown. Then pour new philosophical water over it again, dissolve and coagulate <note>original: *solvirs und coagulirs*; the repetitive cycle of turning a solid into a liquid and back again to purify it</note> it into a powder again, doing this five or six times. This is a sign of the true solution <note>original: *Solution*</note>: when the powder remains at the **bottom** <note>original: *fundo*</note> like sugar; it dissolves

--- Page 147 ---
<meta>This page concludes the instructions for the "Potable Gold" and describes the final transmutation of mercury into a fixed, medicinal oil. It ends with the "Augmentation" process for multiplying the alchemical Stone and a closing prayer.</meta>

...[it dissolves] itself in common water or wine and in all liquids <term>liquids; original Latin: *liquoribus*</term>; then it is the **incombustible oil of the philosophers** <term>incombustible oil; original: *oleum incombustibile*, a legendary substance that can withstand the highest heat without evaporating</term> and the **sugar of the sun and moon** <term>sugar of the sun and moon; original: *saccharum solunae*, referring to the essential salts or concentrated extracts of gold (the sun) and silver (the moon)</term>. It is one single work, distinguished only by this: that the gold takes twice as much time in the work; and it is the highest medicine for the human body, such that nothing is above it <note>original Latin: *ut nihil supra*</note>. Take the sugar of the sun and moon, dissolve it into an oil in warm manure <note>original: *Mist*; manure piles were used by alchemists as a reliable source of gentle, steady heat for the process of "digestion"</note>, then coagulate <term>coagulate; to thicken or solidify a liquid</term> it. Then take one part of the oil and four parts of purified mercury <term>purified mercury; original: *mercurii purgati*</term>; place it in a crucible over the fire, and when it begins to smoke away, pour the oil upon it. Thus the mercury "kills" itself <note>in alchemy, to "kill" mercury means to strip it of its liquid, volatile nature and transform it into a solid, stable metal</note>. Now take it down and pour more upon it, so it becomes fixed and fluid and continues to coagulate other mercury; and you can now multiply it infinitely.

### Augmentation <term>Augmentation; original: *Augmentatio*, the stage of the alchemical process where the power and quantity of the "Stone" are increased</term>.

Take the true and white stone and dissolve it by itself in its own water so that the volatile part exceeds the white fixed part, so that it all becomes volatile. Afterward, create the ferment <term>ferment; original: *Fermentum*, a substance added to "ripen" or complete the alchemical matter</term> in such a way that the greater or fixed part exceeds the size of the volatile. Fix these together, and thus you have multiplied and improved the Stone and obtained princely gifts.
God shall be praised always and forever, Amen.
